The result set is not fixed; it is *computed* based on scope, intent, constraints, and relevance.\\n\\nThink of it less like a database query, and more like **set algebra over meaning**.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### The Mental Model: Context as Sets\\n\\nEvery document you ingest belongs to one or more **sets**.\\n\\nThese sets are defined implicitly by:\\n\\n* `groupName`\\n* metadata fields\\n* semantic similarity\\n* recency and relevance signals\\n\\nAt query time, Alchemyst performs a series of **set operations** to determine what survives into the final context window.\\n\\nConceptually:\\n\\n```\\nFinal Context\\n  = (Semantic Matches)\\n    ∩ (groupName Scope)\\n    ∩ (Metadata Filters)\\n    − (Deduplicated / Superseded Content)\\n```\\n\\nThis is context arithmetic.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Primitive Operations\\n\\nAt a high level, Alchemyst applies four core operations.\\n\\n#### 1. **Intersection (AND)**\\n\\nUsed to **narrow scope**.\\n\\nWhen you specify multiple `groupName` values, *all* of them must match.\\n\\n```javascript theme={null}\\nsearch({\\n  groupName: [\\\"engineering\\\", \\\"backend\\\", \\\"auth\\\"]\\n})\\n```\\n\\nThis is equivalent to:\\n\\n```\\nengineering ∩ backend ∩ auth\\n```\\n\\nOnly documents that belong to **all three sets** survive.\\n\\nThis is why `groupName` is composable—and why flat or overloaded tags break down quickly.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 2. **Union (OR)**\\n\\nUsed implicitly when:\\n\\n* searching semantically\\n* retrieving top-K results\\n* expanding related context\\n\\nFor example, a query like:\\n\\n> “How does authentication work?”\\n\\nmay retrieve documents from:\\n\\n* design docs\\n* code\\n* past PRs\\n* resolved incidents\\n\\nThese are **unioned**, then ranked.\\n\\n```\\n(Auth Docs ∪ Auth Code ∪ Auth Tickets ∪ Auth PRs)\\n```\\n\\nUnion increases recall; intersection increases precision.\\n\\nGood context arithmetic balances both.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 3. **Subtraction (EXCLUSION)**\\n\\nUsed to **remove noise and conflicts**.\\n\\nCommon examples:\\n\\n* Older versions of the same document\\n* Superseded policies\\n* Duplicate or near-duplicate chunks\\n* Out-of-scope access groups\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```\\nAll Auth Docs\\n− Deprecated Docs\\n− Draft Versions\\n− Private/Internal Scope\\n```\\n\\nThis is why deduplication, versioning, and delete-then-add semantics matter—they directly affect arithmetic correctness.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 4. **Ranking (WEIGHTING)**\\n\\nOnce the candidate set is computed, Alchemyst ranks what remains.\\n\\nRanking signals typically include:\\n\\n* semantic similarity\\n* recency\\n* metadata signals (priority, freshness)\\n* historical usefulness\\n* proximity to the query intent\\n\\nOnly the **top-ranked** results are passed to the model.\\n\\nThis is where cost, latency, and answer quality converge.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Why This Matters\\n\\nUnderstanding context arithmetic explains *why* the patterns in this guide exist.\\n\\n* Why hierarchical `groupName` works\\n* Why over-segmentation hurts retrieval\\n* Why metadata bloat slows everything down\\n* Why bulk ingestion and deduplication are not optional\\n* Why document size matters more than raw token count\\n\\nIf your context structure aligns with arithmetic rules, retrieval feels “magical.”\\nIf it doesn’t, no amount of prompt engineering will save you.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### A Simple Example\\n\\nAssume your system contains **50,000 documents**.\\n\\nA user asks:\\n\\n> “How do we handle JWT refresh tokens in API v2?”\\n\\nThe effective context computation might look like:\\n\\n```\\n(\\n  SemanticMatches(\\\"JWT refresh token\\\")\\n)\\n∩ groupName([\\\"engineering\\\", \\\"backend\\\", \\\"auth\\\"])\\n∩ metadata({ version: \\\"v2\\\" })\\n− deprecatedDocs\\n− supersededVersions\\n→ rank → top 5\\n```\\n\\nThe model never sees:\\n\\n* frontend docs\\n* marketing material\\n* outdated auth specs\\n* irrelevant code paths\\n\\nNot because you filtered manually—but because the **arithmetic worked**.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Context Arithmetic vs Prompt Stuffing\\n\\n| Prompt Stuffing | Context Arithmetic |\\n| --------------- | ------------------ |\\n| Static          | Dynamic            |\\n| Manual          | Computed           |\\n| Token-heavy     | Precision-first    |\\n| Fragile         | Composable         |\\n| Hard to scale   | Designed for scale |\\n\\nPrompt stuffing assumes *more tokens = better answers*.\\n\\nContext arithmetic assumes:\\n\\n> *Only the right tokens matter.*\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Design Principle\\n\\n> **Structure your context so that correct answers fall out naturally from arithmetic—not from clever prompts.**\\n\\nIf you internalize this section, every pattern below will feel less like a rulebook and more like common sense.\\n\"}]"},{"role":"assistant","content":"<think>\nThe doc mentions `groupName` (The\n</think>\n\ncamelCase) and uses // Search within specific groups const { contexts } = await client.v1.context.search({ query: \\\"refund policy\\\", scope: 'internal', metadata: { groupName: ['customer-support'] // ← For search (note: camelCase) } }); console.log(`Found ${contexts?.length} results in customer-support`); ``` ```python Python theme={null} # Store with groups client.v1.context.add( documents=[{ \\\"content\\\": \\\"Refund policy: We offer a 30-day money back guarantee...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": { \\\"file_name\\\": \\\"refund-policy.md\\\", \\\"group_name\\\": [\\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"] # ← For storage } }], context_type=\\\"resource\\\", source=\\\"documentation\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\" ) # Search within specific groups result = client.v1.context.search( query=\\\"refund policy\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\", metadata={ \\\"group_name\\\": [\\\"customer-support\\\"] # ← For search (note: snake_case) } ) print(f\\\"Found {len(result.contexts or [])} results in customer-support\\\") ``` \\n\\n **Naming difference:** Storage uses `group_name` (snake\\\\_case in metadata) but TypeScript search uses `groupName` (camelCase). Python uses `group_name` consistently. Both refer to the same field - this is due to API design conventions. \\n\\n### Why use groups?\\n\\n| Benefit                 | Description                                             |\\n| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |\\n| **Faster searches**     | Smaller search space = lower latency                    |\\n| **More relevant**       | Only search customer-support docs, not engineering docs |\\n| **Better organization** | Hierarchical structure like folders                     |\\n| **Access control**      | Filter by team, project, or user permissions            |\\n\\n**Example hierarchy:**\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"]\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"engineering\\\", \\\"api-docs\\\"]\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"hr\\\", \\\"employee-handbook\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nLearn more: [Context Arithmetic](/advanced/context-arithmetic)\\n\\n***\\n\\n## Troubleshooting\\n\\n  **Error symptoms:** * `contexts?.length` returns 0 * Empty array in search results **Common causes:** 1. `similarity_threshold` is too high 2. Document wasn't stored successfully 3. `scope` mismatch between add and search **Fixes:** **Step 1: Lower the threshold:** ```typescript theme={null} similarity_threshold: 0.5 // Instead of 0.9 ``` **Step 2: Verify documents were stored:** ```typescript theme={null} const stored = await client.v1.context.view(); console.log(\\\"Total documents:\\\", stored.length); console.log(\\\"Documents:\\\", stored); ``` **Expected output:** ``` Total documents: 1 Documents: [{ id: \\\"doc_123\\\", content: \\\"Our refund policy...\\\", ... }] ``` **Step 3: Check scope matches:** ```typescript theme={null} // Both must use the same scope await client.v1.context.add({ scope: 'internal', ... }); await client.v1.context.search({ scope: 'internal', ... }); // ✅ Match ```   **Error symptoms:** * Getting documents that don't match the query * Too many results to process efficiently **Common causes:** 1. `similarity_threshold` is too low 2. Documents are too broad or generic 3. Not using group filtering **Fixes:** **Step 1: Raise the threshold:** ```typescript theme={null} similarity_threshold: 0.8 // More strict (was 0.5) ``` **Step 2: Use group filtering:** ```typescript theme={null} metadata: { groupName: ['customer-support'] // Narrow to specific category } ``` **Step 3: Add more specific metadata:** ```typescript theme={null} await client.v1.context.add({ documents: [{ content: \\\"...\\\", metadata: { group_name: [\\\"support\\\", \\\"refunds\\\"], // More specific category: \\\"policies\\\", department: \\\"customer-service\\\" } }] }); ```   **Error message:** ```json theme={null} { \\\"error\\\": \\\"Document with file_name 'policy.md' already exists\\\", \\\"code\\\": \\\"CONFLICT\\\", \\\"status\\\": 409 } ``` **Cause:** Trying to add a document with duplicate `file_name` in metadata. **Fixes:** **Step 1: Delete old version first:** ```typescript theme={null} // Delete by file name await client.v1.context.delete({ metadata: { fileName: \\\"policy.md\\\" } }); // Then add new version await client.v1.context.add({ documents: [{ content: \\\"Updated content...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"policy.md\\\" } }] }); ``` **Step 2: Use versioned names:** ```typescript theme={null} file_name: \\\"policy-v2.md\\\" // or \\\"policy-2024-02-01.md\\\" ``` **Step 3: Use unique identifiers:** ```typescript theme={null} file_name: `policy-${Date.now()}.md` // Timestamp // or file_name: `policy-${uuid()}.md` // UUID ```   **Error message:** ```json theme={null} { \\\"error\\\": \\\"Invalid API key\\\", \\\"code\\\": \\\"UNAUTHORIZED\\\", \\\"status\\\": 401 } ``` **Fixes:** **Step 1: Verify API key is set:** ```typescript theme={null} console.log(\\\"API Key exists:\\\", !!process.env.ALCHEMYST_AI_API_KEY); The result set is not fixed; it is *computed* based on scope, intent, constraints, and relevance.\\n\\nThink of it less like a database query, and more like **set algebra over meaning**.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### The Mental Model: Context as Sets\\n\\nEvery document you ingest belongs to one or more **sets**.\\n\\nThese sets are defined implicitly by:\\n\\n* `groupName`\\n* metadata fields\\n* semantic similarity\\n* recency and relevance signals\\n\\nAt query time, Alchemyst performs a series of **set operations** to determine what survives into the final context window.\\n\\nConceptually:\\n\\n```\\nFinal Context\\n  = (Semantic Matches)\\n    ∩ (groupName Scope)\\n    ∩ (Metadata Filters)\\n    − (Deduplicated / Superseded Content)\\n```\\n\\nThis is context arithmetic.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Primitive Operations\\n\\nAt a high level, Alchemyst applies four core operations.\\n\\n#### 1. **Intersection (AND)**\\n\\nUsed to **narrow scope**.\\n\\nWhen you specify multiple `groupName` values, *all* of them must match.\\n\\n```javascript theme={null}\\nsearch({\\n  groupName: [\\\"engineering\\\", \\\"backend\\\", \\\"auth\\\"]\\n})\\n```\\n\\nThis is equivalent to:\\n\\n```\\nengineering ∩ backend ∩ auth\\n```\\n\\nOnly documents that belong to **all three sets** survive.\\n\\nThis is why `groupName` is composable—and why flat or overloaded tags break down quickly.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 2. **Union (OR)**\\n\\nUsed implicitly when:\\n\\n* searching semantically\\n* retrieving top-K results\\n* expanding related context\\n\\nFor example, a query like:\\n\\n> “How does authentication work?”\\n\\nmay retrieve documents from:\\n\\n* design docs\\n* code\\n* past PRs\\n* resolved incidents\\n\\nThese are **unioned**, then ranked.\\n\\n```\\n(Auth Docs ∪ Auth Code ∪ Auth Tickets ∪ Auth PRs)\\n```\\n\\nUnion increases recall; intersection increases precision.\\n\\nGood context arithmetic balances both.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 3. **Subtraction (EXCLUSION)**\\n\\nUsed to **remove noise and conflicts**.\\n\\nCommon examples:\\n\\n* Older versions of the same document\\n* Superseded policies\\n* Duplicate or near-duplicate chunks\\n* Out-of-scope access groups\\n\\nFor example:\\n\\n```\\nAll Auth Docs\\n− Deprecated Docs\\n− Draft Versions\\n− Private/Internal Scope\\n```\\n\\nThis is why deduplication, versioning, and delete-then-add semantics matter—they directly affect arithmetic correctness.\\n\\n***\\n\\n#### 4. **Ranking (WEIGHTING)**\\n\\nOnce the candidate set is computed, Alchemyst ranks what remains.\\n\\nRanking signals typically include:\\n\\n* semantic similarity\\n* recency\\n* metadata signals (priority, freshness)\\n* historical usefulness\\n* proximity to the query intent\\n\\nOnly the **top-ranked** results are passed to the model.\\n\\nThis is where cost, latency, and answer quality converge.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Why This Matters\\n\\nUnderstanding context arithmetic explains *why* the patterns in this guide exist.\\n\\n* Why hierarchical `groupName` works\\n* Why over-segmentation hurts retrieval\\n* Why metadata bloat slows everything down\\n* Why bulk ingestion and deduplication are not optional\\n* Why document size matters more than raw token count\\n\\nIf your context structure aligns with arithmetic rules, retrieval feels “magical.”\\nIf it doesn’t, no amount of prompt engineering will save you.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### A Simple Example\\n\\nAssume your system contains **50,000 documents**.\\n\\nA user asks:\\n\\n> “How do we handle JWT refresh tokens in API v2?”\\n\\nThe effective context computation might look like:\\n\\n```\\n(\\n  SemanticMatches(\\\"JWT refresh token\\\")\\n)\\n∩ groupName([\\\"engineering\\\", \\\"backend\\\", \\\"auth\\\"])\\n∩ metadata({ version: \\\"v2\\\" })\\n− deprecatedDocs\\n− supersededVersions\\n→ rank → top 5\\n```\\n\\nThe model never sees:\\n\\n* frontend docs\\n* marketing material\\n* outdated auth specs\\n* irrelevant code paths\\n\\nNot because you filtered manually—but because the **arithmetic worked**.\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Context Arithmetic vs Prompt Stuffing\\n\\n| Prompt Stuffing | Context Arithmetic |\\n| --------------- | ------------------ |\\n| Static          | Dynamic            |\\n| Manual          | Computed           |\\n| Token-heavy     | Precision-first    |\\n| Fragile         | Composable         |\\n| Hard to scale   | Designed for scale |\\n\\nPrompt stuffing assumes *more tokens = better answers*.\\n\\nContext arithmetic assumes:\\n\\n> *Only the right tokens matter.*\\n\\n***\\n\\n### Design Principle\\n\\n> **Structure your context so that correct answers fall out naturally from arithmetic—not from clever prompts.**\\n\\nIf you internalize this section, every pattern below will feel less like a rulebook and more like common sense.\\n\"}]"},{"role":"assistant","content":"<think>\nThe doc mentions `groupName` (The\n</think>\n\ncamelCase) and uses To request a refund, contact support@example.com. ``` \\n\\n***\\n\\n## Next: Add Real Documents\\n\\nNow that you understand the basics, let's add real documents:\\n\\n   ```typescript TypeScript theme={null} import fs from 'fs'; console.log(\\\"✅ File uploaded and indexed\\\"); ``` ```python Python theme={null} with open('./docs/policy.txt', 'r') as f: content = f.read() client.v1.context.add( documents=[{ \\\"content\\\": content, \\\"metadata\\\": { \\\"file_name\\\": \\\"policy.txt\\\", \\\"file_type\\\": \\\"text\\\" } }], context_type=\\\"resource\\\", source=\\\"documentation\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\" ) print(\\\"✅ File uploaded and indexed\\\") ```     ```typescript TypeScript theme={null} const response = await fetch('https://api.example.com/docs'); const apiDocs = await response.json(); const docs = apiDocs.map(doc => ({ content: doc.body, metadata: { file_name: doc.title, doc_id: doc.id } })); await client.v1.context.add({ documents: docs, context_type: 'resource', source: 'api', scope: 'internal' }); console.log(`✅ Indexed ${docs.length} documents from API`); ``` ```python Python theme={null} import requests response = requests.get('https://api.example.com/docs') api_docs = response.json() docs = [{ \\\"content\\\": doc[\\\"body\\\"], \\\"metadata\\\": { \\\"file_name\\\": doc[\\\"title\\\"], \\\"doc_id\\\": doc[\\\"id\\\"] } } for doc in api_docs] client.v1.context.add( documents=docs, context_type=\\\"resource\\\", source=\\\"api\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\" ) print(f\\\"✅ Indexed {len(docs)} documents from API\\\") ```     ```typescript TypeScript theme={null} const docs = [ { content: \\\"Refund policy: We offer a 30-day money back guarantee...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"refunds.md\\\" } }, { content: \\\"Shipping policy: We ship worldwide within 5-7 business days...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"shipping.md\\\" } }, { content: \\\"Privacy policy: We collect and protect your data...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"privacy.md\\\" } } ]; await client.v1.context.add({ documents: docs, context_type: 'resource', source: 'documentation', scope: 'internal' }); console.log(`✅ Indexed ${docs.length} policy documents`); ``` ```python Python theme={null} docs = [ { \\\"content\\\": \\\"Refund policy: We offer a 30-day money back guarantee...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"file_name\\\": \\\"refunds.md\\\"} }, { \\\"content\\\": \\\"Shipping policy: We ship worldwide within 5-7 business days...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"file_name\\\": \\\"shipping.md\\\"} }, { \\\"content\\\": \\\"Privacy policy: We collect and protect your data...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"file_name\\\": \\\"privacy.md\\\"} } ] client.v1.context.add( documents=docs, context_type=\\\"resource\\\", source=\\\"documentation\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\" ) print(f\\\"✅ Indexed {len(docs)} policy documents\\\") ```  \\n\\n***\\n\\n## Advanced: Organize with Metadata\\n\\nUse `group_name` to filter searches by category:\\n\\n ```typescript TypeScript theme={null} // Store with groups await client.v1.context.add({ documents: [{ content: \\\"Refund policy: We offer a 30-day money back guarantee...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"refund-policy.md\\\", group_name: [\\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"] // ← For storage } }], context_type: 'resource', source: 'documentation', scope: 'internal' }); // Search within specific groups const { contexts } = await client.v1.context.search({ query: \\\"refund policy\\\", scope: 'internal', metadata: { groupName: ['customer-support'] // ← For search (note: camelCase) } }); console.log(`Found ${contexts?.length} results in customer-support`); ``` ```python Python theme={null} # Store with groups client.v1.context.add( documents=[{ \\\"content\\\": \\\"Refund policy: We offer a 30-day money back guarantee...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": { \\\"file_name\\\": \\\"refund-policy.md\\\", \\\"group_name\\\": [\\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"] # ← For storage } }], context_type=\\\"resource\\\", source=\\\"documentation\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\" ) # Search within specific groups result = client.v1.context.search( query=\\\"refund policy\\\", scope=\\\"internal\\\", metadata={ \\\"group_name\\\": [\\\"customer-support\\\"] # ← For search (note: snake_case) } ) print(f\\\"Found {len(result.contexts or [])} results in customer-support\\\") ``` \\n\\n **Naming difference:** Storage uses `group_name` (snake\\\\_case in metadata) but TypeScript search uses `groupName` (camelCase). Python uses `group_name` consistently. Both refer to the same field - this is due to API design conventions. \\n\\n### Why use groups?\\n\\n| Benefit                 | Description                                             |\\n| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |\\n| **Faster searches**     | Smaller search space = lower latency                    |\\n| **More relevant**       | Only search customer-support docs, not engineering docs |\\n| **Better organization** | Hierarchical structure like folders                     |\\n| **Access control**      | Filter by team, project, or user permissions            |\\n\\n**Example hierarchy:**\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"]\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"engineering\\\", \\\"api-docs\\\"]\\ngroup_name: [\\\"company\\\", \\\"hr\\\", \\\"employee-handbook\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nLearn more: [Context Arithmetic](/advanced/context-arithmetic)\\n\\n***\\n\\n## Troubleshooting\\n\\n  **Error symptoms:** * `contexts?.length` returns 0 * Empty array in search results **Common causes:** 1. `similarity_threshold` is too high 2. Document wasn't stored successfully 3. `scope` mismatch between add and search **Fixes:** **Step 1: Lower the threshold:** ```typescript theme={null} similarity_threshold: 0.5 // Instead of 0.9 ``` **Step 2: Verify documents were stored:** ```typescript theme={null} const stored = await client.v1.context.view(); console.log(\\\"Total documents:\\\", stored.length); console.log(\\\"Documents:\\\", stored); ``` **Expected output:** ``` Total documents: 1 Documents: [{ id: \\\"doc_123\\\", content: \\\"Our refund policy...\\\", ... }] ``` **Step 3: Check scope matches:** ```typescript theme={null} // Both must use the same scope await client.v1.context.add({ scope: 'internal', ... }); await client.v1.context.search({ scope: 'internal', ... }); // ✅ Match ```   **Error symptoms:** * Getting documents that don't match the query * Too many results to process efficiently **Common causes:** 1. `similarity_threshold` is too low 2. Documents are too broad or generic 3. Not using group filtering **Fixes:** **Step 1: Raise the threshold:** ```typescript theme={null} similarity_threshold: 0.8 // More strict (was 0.5) ``` **Step 2: Use group filtering:** ```typescript theme={null} metadata: { groupName: ['customer-support'] // Narrow to specific category } ``` **Step 3: Add more specific metadata:** ```typescript theme={null} await client.v1.context.add({ documents: [{ content: \\\"...\\\", metadata: { group_name: [\\\"support\\\", \\\"refunds\\\"], // More specific category: \\\"policies\\\", department: \\\"customer-service\\\" } }] }); ```   **Error message:** ```json theme={null} { \\\"error\\\": \\\"Document with file_name 'policy.md' already exists\\\", \\\"code\\\": \\\"CONFLICT\\\", \\\"status\\\": 409 } ``` **Cause:** Trying to add a document with duplicate `file_name` in metadata. **Fixes:** **Step 1: Delete old version first:** ```typescript theme={null} // Delete by file name await client.v1.context.delete({ metadata: { fileName: \\\"policy.md\\\" } }); // Then add new version await client.v1.context.add({ documents: [{ content: \\\"Updated content...\\\", metadata: { file_name: \\\"policy.md\\\" } }] }); ``` **Step 2: Use versioned names:** ```typescript theme={null} file_name: \\\"policy-v2.md\\\" // or \\\"policy-2024-02-01.md\\\" ``` **Step 3: Use unique identifiers:** ```typescript theme={null} file_name: `policy-${Date.now()}.md` // Timestamp // or file_name: `policy-${uuid()}.md` // UUID ```   **Error message:** ```json theme={null} { \\\"error\\\": \\\"Invalid API key\\\", \\\"code\\\": \\\"UNAUTHORIZED\\\", \\\"status\\\": 401 } ``` **Fixes:** **Step 1: Verify API key is set:** ```typescript theme={null} console.log(\\\"API Key exists:\\\", !!process.env.ALCHEMYST_AI_API_KEY); // Should output: API Key exists: true ``` **Step 2: Check key format:** ```typescript theme={null} // Should start with \\\"alch_\\\" or similar prefix console.log(\\\"Key prefix:\\\", process.env.ALCHEMYST_AI_API_KEY?.substring(0, 5)); ``` **Step 3: Get a new key:** * Visit [platform.getalchemystai.com/settings](https://platform.getalchemystai.com/settings?utm_source=docs\\\\&utm_campaign=contextual_ai_quickstart\\\\&utm_medium=quickstart_article\\\\&utm_content=troubleshooting_api_key_settings) * Generate a new API key * Update your `.env` file   **Error message:** ```json theme={null} { \\\"error\\\": \\\"Rate limit exceeded\\\", \\\"code\\\": \\\"RATE_LIMIT\\\", \\\"status\\\": 429, \\\"retry_after\\\": 60 } ``` **Cause:** Too many requests in a short time period. **Fixes:** **Step 1: Add retry logic:** ```typescript theme={null} async function searchWithRetry(query: string, retries = 3) { for (let i = 0; i < retries; i++) { try { return await client.v1.context.search({ query, scope: 'internal' }); } catch (error) { if (error.status === 429 && i < retries - 1) { const waitTime = error.retry_after || 60; console.log(`Rate limited. Waiting ${waitTime}s...`); await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, waitTime * 1000)); } else { throw error; } } } } ``` **Step 2: Check your rate limits:** * Free tier: 100 operations/day * Pro tier: 10,000 operations/day **Step 3: Upgrade your plan:** [View pricing](https://platform.getalchemystai.com/settings/billing?utm_source=docs\\\\&utm_campaign=contextual_ai_quickstart\\\\&utm_medium=quickstart_article\\\\&utm_content=troubleshooting_rate_limit_billing) \\n\\n***\\n\\n## Best Practices\\n\\n### Document Organization\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\n// ✅ Good - organized with meaningful metadata\\nawait client.v1.context.add({\\n  documents: [{\\n    content: \\\"...\\\",\\n    metadata: {\\n      file_name: \\\"refund-policy-2024-02.md\\\",\\n      group_name: [\\\"customer-support\\\", \\\"policies\\\"],\\n      department: \\\"support\\\",\\n      last_updated: \\\"2024-02-01\\\"\\n    }\\n  }]\\n});\\n\\n// ❌ Bad - minimal metadata, hard to search\\nawait client.v1.context.add({\\n  documents: [{\\n    content: \\\"...\\\"\\n  }]\\n});\\n```\\n\\n### Chunk Size Considerations\\n\\n* **Small documents** (under 1000 words): Add as-is\\n* **Medium documents** (1000-5000 words): Let Alchemyst REDACTED-chunk\\n* **Large documents** (over 5000 words): Consider splitting by section\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\n// For large documents, split manually for better control\\nconst sections = [\\n  { title: \\\"Introduction\\\", content: \\\"...\\\" },\\n  { title: \\\"Features\\\", content: \\\"...\\\" },\\n  { title: \\\"Pricing\\\", content: \\\"...\\\" }\\n];\\n\\nconst docs = sections.map(section => ({\\n  content: section.content,\\n  metadata: {\\n    file_name: \\\"product-docs.md\\\",\\n    section: section.title\\n  }\\n}));\\n\\nawait client.v1.context.add({ documents: docs });\\n```\\n\\n### Search Optimization\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\n// ✅ Good - specific query with filtering\\nconst { contexts } = await client.v1.context.search({\\n  query: \\\"How to request a refund for damaged items\\\",\\n  scope: 'internal',\\n  similarity_threshold: 0.7,\\n  metadata: {\\n    groupName: ['customer-support', 'refunds']\\n  }\\n});\\n\\n// ❌ Bad - too broad, no filtering\\nconst { contexts } = await client.v1.context.search({\\n  query: \\\"help\\\",\\n  scope: 'internal'\\n});\\n```\\n\\n### Error Handling\\n\\n```typescript theme={null}\\nasync function safeSearch(query: string) {\\n  try {\\n    const { contexts } = await client.v1.context.search({\\n      query,\\n      scope: 'internal',\\n      similarity_threshold: 0.7\\n    });\\n    \\n    if (!contexts || contexts.length === 0) {\\n      console.log(\\\"No results found. Try a broader query.\\\");\\n      return null;\\n    }\\n    \\n    return contexts;\\n  } catch (error) {\\n    console.error(\\\"Search failed:\\\", error.message);\\n    // Fallback or retry logic here\\n    return null;\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n***\\n\\n## Verify Your Setup\\n\\nAfter implementing context search, verify everything is working:\\n\\n### Check Platform UI\\n\\n1.
